[[UFC Live: Sanchez vs. Kampmann]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Nogueira vs. Davis]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 128: Shogun vs. Jones''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[pay-per-view]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on March 19, 2011, at the [[Prudential Center]] in [[Newark, New Jersey]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC128#/fight |title=UFC 128: Shogun vs. Jones|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=2011-01-04}}{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-128-full-lineup-030511 |title=Full UFC 128 Fight Card Announced |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|date=March 9, 2011}}\n\n==Background==\nThis event was expected to take place in [[Abu Dhabi]], the promotion's first return to the [[United Arab Emirates]] since [[UFC 112]].{{cite web |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/11/10/1806786/ufc-returns-to-abu-dhabi-for-ufc-128-on-march-19th |title=UFC Returns to Abu Dhabi for UFC 128 on March 19th |publisher=bloodyelbow.com |date=November 10, 2010 |access-date=November 11, 2010 |archive-date=July 8, 2011 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110708073723/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/11/10/1806786/ufc-returns-to-abu-dhabi-for-ufc-128-on-march-19th |url-status=dead }}{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-128-headed-to-abu-dhabi-on-march-19-2011-shogun-vs-evans-possible-headliner |title=UFC 128 Headed to Abu Dhabi in March; Shogun vs. Evans Possible |publisher=MMAWeekly.com|date=November 10, 2010}} However, the organization scrapped the idea after a suitable location for the event could not be finalized in time, since the temporary venue that hosted [[UFC 112]] had been dismantled.{{cite web |url=http://mmacombine.com/test/news/ufcs-abu-dhabi-return-scrapped-newark-likely-for-march-event/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110714104310/http://mmacombine.com/test/news/ufcs-abu-dhabi-return-scrapped-newark-likely-for-march-event/ |url-status=dead |archive-date=July 14, 2011 |title=UFC's Abu Dhabi Return Scrapped |publisher=mmacombine.com |date=December 16, 2010 }}\n\nA bout between [[Tito Ortiz]] and [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]], was linked to this event, but was moved to headline [[UFC Fight Night 24]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21822/antonio-rogerio-nogueira-vs-tito-ortiz-headlines-ufc-fight-night-24-in-seattle.mma |title=Antonio Rogerio Nogueira vs. Tito Ortiz headlines UFC Fight Night 24 in Seattle |publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=December 21, 2010}}\n\nA bout between [[Brendan Schaub]] and [[Frank Mir]] was expected for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21736/brendan-schaub-vs-frank-mir-targeted-for-early-2011-ufc-event.mma|title=Frank Mir vs. Brendan Schaub also Stefan Struve vs. Brendan Schaub was targeted for early-2011 UFC event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=December 13, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120710105350/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21736/brendan-schaub-vs-frank-mir-targeted-for-early-2011-ufc-event.mma|archive-date=July 10, 2012}} However, Schaub ended up fighting [[Mirko Cro Cop]].{{cite web |url=http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=5850 |title=Filipovic vs. Schaub at UFC 128 |publisher=fightersonly.com |date=December 28, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110102092035/http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=5850 |archive-date=January 2, 2011 }}\n\nA bout between [[Chael Sonnen]] and [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]] was briefly reported but Sonnen pleaded guilty to federal charges of money laundering{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21966/despite-legal-issues-sonnen-vs-akiyama-in-the-works-for-ufc-128.mma|title=Guilty plea shadows Chael Sonnen vs. Yoshihiro Akiyama at UFC 128|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110107071706/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21966/despite-legal-issues-sonnen-vs-akiyama-in-the-works-for-ufc-128.mma|archive-date=2011-01-07}} and had his contract placed on hold by the UFC.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21976/following-money-laundering-conviction-chael-sonnen-suspended-by-ufc.mma|title=Following money laundering conviction, Chael Sonnen suspended by UFC|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120712052312/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21976/following-money-laundering-conviction-chael-sonnen-suspended-by-ufc.mma|archive-date=2012-07-12}} [[Nate Marquardt]] was then signed to compete against Akiyama.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=5995483|title=Sources: Marquardt vs. Akiyama at 128|author=Brett Okamoto|work=[[ESPN]]|date=January 6, 2011}}{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=6209527|title = Japan's Akiyama pulls out of UFC 128; Miller in|date = 12 March 2011}} However, on March 12, it was announced that Yoshihiro Akiyama had to pull out of his fight with Nate Marquardt due to the [[2011 Tōhoku earthquake and tsunami|tragedy in Japan]]. [[Dana White]] then announced via Twitter that [[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] would be stepping up from the preliminary fights to replace Akiyama on the main card.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22803/yoshihiro-akiyama-out-at-ufc-128-dan-miller-now-faces-nate-marquardt.mma|title=Yoshihiro Akiyama out at UFC 128; Dan Miller now faces Nate Marquardt|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=March 12, 2011}} Miller's original opponent [[Nick Catone]] instead faced UFC newcomer [[Costas Philippou]] in a 195-pound catchweight bout.\n\nDuring [[Jon Jones]]' post-fight interview following his victory over [[Ryan Bader]] at [[UFC 126]], [[Joe Rogan]] announced that [[Rashad Evans]] had injured his knee during training. Jones was then informed that the UFC wanted him to replace Evans in a fight against [[Maurício Rua]] in the main event for the [[List of UFC champions#Light Heavyweight Championship|UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]]. Jones accepted, and the fight was set for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22361/rashad-evans-hurt-jon-jones-now-challenges-shogun-rua-for-title-at-ufc-128.mma|title=Rashad Evans hurt, Jon Jones now challenges Shogun Rua for title at UFC 128|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 5, 2011|access-date=February 6, 2011|archive-date=May 25, 2012|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525142117/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22361/rashad-evans-hurt-jon-jones-now-challenges-shogun-rua-for-title-at-ufc-128.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] was originally offered the fight before Jon Jones. He declined the fight saying he would never take a fight with only a four-week notice. He also stated that he currently weighed 250 pounds and would have to lose 45 pounds in that four week time period and would not have time to actually train.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22383/rampage-jackson-cites-poor-timing-weight-for-ufc-128-title-fight-decline.mma|title=\"Rampage\" Jackson cites poor timing, weight for UFC 128 title-fight decline|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 7, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110209080308/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22383/rampage-jackson-cites-poor-timing-weight-for-ufc-128-title-fight-decline.mma|archive-date=February 9, 2011}}\n\n[[Karlos Vemola]] was scheduled to face [[Luiz Cane]] on the card, but Vemola was forced out of the fight with an injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22568/karlos-vemola-out-luiz-cane-without-opponent-for-ufc-128.mma|title=Karlos Vemola out, Luiz Cane without opponent for UFC 128|date=February 22, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110225232752/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22568/karlos-vemola-out-luiz-cane-without-opponent-for-ufc-128.mma|archive-date=February 25, 2011}} [[Eliot Marshall]] stepped in and replaced Vemola.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22578/eliot-marshall-returns-steps-in-to-meet-luiz-cane-at-ufc-128.mma|title=Eliot Marshall returns, steps in to meet Luiz Cane at UFC 128|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 23, 2011}}\n\n[[Manvel Gamburyan]] was scheduled to take on [[Raphael Assunção]], but withdrew due to a back injury on February 25.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2011/02/25/manny-gamburyan-pulls-out-of-ufc-128/|title=Manny Gamburyan Pulls Out Of UFC 128|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=February 25, 2011}}{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22606/featherweight-manny-gamburyan-out-at-ufc-128-in-new-jersey.mma |title=Featherweight Manny Gamburyan out at UFC 128 in New Jersey |publisher=mmajunkie.com faced Assunção, as Koch's original opponent for the [[UFC on Versus 3]] event two weeks prior to UFC 128, [[Cub Swanson]], pulled out due to injury around the same time as Gamburyan.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/With-Opponents-Injured-Koch-Assuncao-Tabbed-for-UFC-128-30445|title=With opponents injured, Koch/Assuncao tabbed for UFC States\n|network = [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]]\n|first_aired = {{start date|2011|3|30}}\n|last_aired =\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Lesnar vs. Team dos Santos''''' is the thirteenth installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.\n\nThe UFC and Spike TV held open tryouts on November 4, 2010, in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]]. The casting call went out for [[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]] and [[Middleweight (MMA)|Middleweight]] fighters, however, all fighters will be in the welterweight division.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22040/the-ultimate-fighter-13-cast-shifts-to-all-welterweights-coaches-named-this-week.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 13\" cast shifts to all welterweights, coaches named this week|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=January 10, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110112001821/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22040/the-ultimate-fighter-13-cast-shifts-to-all-welterweights-coaches-named-this-week.mma|archive-date=January 12, 2011}} All fighters applying and trying out for the show must have at least three professional fights and be at least 21 years of age.{{cite web |url=http://www.MMAMania.com/2010/10/13/1748732/the-ultimate-fighter-tuf-13-open-casting-call-from-spike-tv-and-ufc|title=The Ultimate Fighter (TUF 13) open casting call from Spike TV and UFC|publisher=mmamania.com|date=October 13, 2010}} Notables who turned up to the tryouts included [[Strikeforce (mixed martial arts)|Strikeforce]] veterans [[Karl Amoussou]], [[Andre Galvao]], and [[Zak Cummings]], and former TUF contestants [[Frank Lester (fighter)|Frank Lester]] and [[Jeremiah Riggs]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21283/the-ultimate-fighter-13-tryouts-draw-several-notables-including-galvao-amoussou-toney.mma|title=The Ultimate Fighter 13 tryouts draw several notables|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=November 5, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101106223639/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21283/the-ultimate-fighter-13-tryouts-draw-several-notables-including-galvao-amoussou-toney.mma|archive-date=November 6, 2010}}\n\nIt was confirmed during the [[UFC Fight Night 23|Fight for the Troops 2]] conference call that [[Brock Lesnar]] and [[Junior dos Santos]] would be the coaches for this season.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/tuf-13-coaching-announcement|title=TUF 13 Coaches Announced|publisher=ufc.com|date=January 11, 2011}}\n\nThis season did not feature an elimination round like the two seasons before it, however, the \"wild card\" selections introduced in [[The Ultimate Fighter 11|season 11]] were still featured. The initial cast was 14 fighters rather than the usual 16, 28 or 32 in recent seasons.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22203/the-ultimate-fighter-13-eliminates-elimination-round-initial-cast-down-to-14.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 13\" eliminates elimination round, initial cast down to 14|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=January 24, 2011}}\n\nOn May 12, 2011, midway through the airing of the season, [[Dana White]] announced that [[Brock Lesnar]] pulled out of the coaches' fight due to the return of his [[diverticulitis]].{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/brock-lesnar-fighting-diverticulitus-again-shane-carwin-steps-up |title=Brock Lesnar Fighting Diverticulitus Again; Shane Carwin Steps Up |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=2011-05-12}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n;{{color box|#237049|border=silver}} Team Lesnar:\n*[[Brock Lesnar]]\n*Marty Morgan\n*[[Erik Paulson]]\n*[[Rodrigo Medeiros]]\n*Greg Nelson\n*Chris Manos\n*[[Matt Hughes (fighter)|Matt Hughes]] (guest coach)\n\n;{{color box|#9AABA6|border=silver}} Team dos Santos:\n*Junior dos Santos\n*Antonio Peinado\n*Billy Scheibe{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/junior-dos-santos-makes-his-choices-for-tuf-13-assistant-coaches/|title=Junior Dos Santos Makes His Choices for TUF 13 Assistant Coaches|publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=January 20, 2011}}\n*Lew Polley\n*[[Luiz Dorea]]\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Lesnar'''\n:* Len Bentley, Charlie Rader, [[Tony Ferguson (fighter)|Tony Ferguson]], [[Clay Harvison]], [[Chris Cope]], Nordin Asrih, [[Myles Jury]] (Chuck O'Neil)*\n*'''Team dos Santos'''\n:* [[Shamar Bailey]], Ryan McGillivray, Javier Torres, [[Ramsey Nijem]], Zachary Davis, Michael \"Mick\" Bowman, Keon Caldwell ([[Justin Edwards (fighter)|Justin Edwards]])*\n\n''* Jury was replaced by O'Neil due to injury.''\n\n''* Caldwell quit the show and was replaced by Edwards.''\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1: Something to Prove'''\n*Dana White welcomed 14 fighters to the UFC training facility and announced that all 14 would automatically get into the house. He also announced the return of the wildcard format.\n*The coaches for the season were introduced as Brock Lesnar and Junior dos Santos. They then went on to evaluate the fighters.\n*During the evaluations, Jury got injured and was checked out by a doctor, who said that nothing was broken but still wanted to do an MRI.\n*Both coaches met in White's office to go through the fighter picks via a coin toss (green for Lesnar, grey for dos Santos). The coin toss was won by Lesnar who chose to pick the first fighter, leaving dos Santos with first choice of matches.\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n|-\n!Lesnar\n|Len Bentley\n|Charlie Rader\n|Tony Ferguson\n|Clay Harvison\n|Myles Jury\n|Chris Cope\n|Nordin Asrih\n|-\n!Dos Santos\n|Shamar Bailey\n|Ryan McGillivray\n|Javier Torres\n|Ramsey Nijem\n|Zachary Davis\n|Mick Bowman\n|Keon Caldwell\n|}\n*Jury was told that he had a torn ACL and was forced off the show. White promised that he would get a second shot into the UFC after he heals up.\n*Jury was replaced by Chuck O'Neil.\n*Dos Santos announced that Bailey will face Asrih.\n*Both fighters speak of their religious backgrounds, as Bailey is the son of a pastor and Asrih is a German-born Muslim.\n*'''Shamar Bailey''' defeated Nordin Asrih by unanimous decision after two rounds.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23043/episode-no-1-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 1 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=March 30, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 2: Suck It Up'''\n*Keon Caldwell struggled during an intense Team dos Santos training session and vomited. He told dos Santos that it was hard for him to be away from his family.\n*Lesnar admitted that he expected to lose the first fight between his seventh pick Nordin Asrih and Team dos Santos' top pick Shamar Bailey.\n*Caldwell told dos Santos the next day that he wanted to go home. Dos Santos, and later White, encourage to him to keep going. After initially agreeing to continue, Caldwell later changed his mind and left the show.\n*Dos Santos announced that Javier Torres would face Chris Cope.\n*'''Chris Cope''' defeated Javier Torres via unanimous decision after three rounds.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23119/episode-no-2-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 2 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=April 6, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 3: Chicken Salad'''\n*Lesnar's \"making chicken salad out of chicken shit\" comment confused his team, but he recovered by telling his fighters they became chicken salad once they had made it onto the show, and he also told them to stay motivated.\n*[[Justin Edwards (fighter)|Justin Edwards]], a former [[Bellator MMA]] fighter and 6–0 in his career, arrived to replace Keon Caldwell on Team dos Santos.\n*Dos Santos' wrestling coach, Lew Polley, ran an intense training session that eventually resulted in Shamar Bailey being cut above the eye.\n*Lesnar announced that Len Bentley would face Ryan McGillivray.\n*Chris Cope was seen hanging out with Team dos Santos a lot, making the rest of the green team wonder what his motives were. Bentley seemed especially suspicious and asked Lesnar to talk to Cope, who told him to calm down and fight.\n*Dissatisfied with Polley's training sessions, dos Santos reminded him that the competition is a team effort, not his own.\n*Four hours prior to weigh-in, McGillivray weighed 180 lb, so he cut the weight in 45 minutes with dos Santos and Polley in an Epsom salt bath. Both fighters made weight at 170 lb.\n*[[Maurício Rua|Maurício \"Shogun\" Rua]] paid a visit to dos Santos in support of the grey team and to watch the fight cageside.\n*Lesnar was absent from the fight for personal reasons.\n*'''Ryan McGillivray''' defeated Len Bentley by majority decision after two rounds.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23224/episode-no-3-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 3 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=April 13, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 4: A Bad Dream'''\n*Lesnar returned after leaving before the Bentley-McGillivray fight and congratulated Len Bentley on his efforts. Lesnar showed a commitment to getting wins from his four remaining fighters yet to compete: Tony Ferguson, Clay Harvison, Chuck O'Neil, and Charlie Rader.\n*Lesnar removed Harvison from a training session momentarily for looking distracted and not committed. He also was angry with Rader for the same reasons.\n*Dos Santos announced that Ramsey Nijem would face Rader.\n*Lesnar invited [[Matt Hughes (fighter)|Matt Hughes]] as a guest instructor for wrestling techniques.\n*'''Ramsey Nijem''' defeated Charlie Rader by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:31 of the second round.{{cite web|url=http://bleacherreport.com/articles/672166-brock-lesnar-vs-junior-dos-santos-tuf-13-live-results-and-analysis-episode-4/entry/67051-brock-lesnar-vs-jds-tuf-13-on-spike-tv-ramsey-nijem-defeats-charlie-rader|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110426110425/http://bleacherreport.com/articles/672166-brock-lesnar-vs-junior-dos-santos-tuf-13-live-results-and-analysis-episode-4/entry/67051-brock-lesnar-vs-jds-tuf-13-on-spike-tv-ramsey-nijem-defeats-charlie-rader|url-status=dead|archive-date=April 26, 2011|title=Brock Lesnar vs. JDS: TUF 13 on Spike TV – Ramsey Nijem Defeats Charlie Rader|publisher=Bleacher Report|date=April 20, 2011}}\n*Lesnar and O'Neil were visibly upset. Inside the dressing room, Lesnar began to scold the team, prompting Bentley to storm out of the room.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23304/episode-no-4-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 4 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=April 20, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 5: Wow Me'''\n*As Lesnar continued to scold his team after Charlie Rader's loss to Ramsey Nijem, Len Bentley came back into the room, and Lesnar questioned his toughness. He said that Chris Cope (the only fighter on the green team to win his bout through four preliminary fights) is the only one that has impressed him so far.\n*Back at the house, Bentley continued to vent his frustration and showed his dislike of Lesnar.\n*Dos Santos announced that Mick Bowman would face Clay Harvison.\n*At a Team dos Santos training session, dos Santos was drilling boxing techniques when Lew Polley took over, leaving dos Santos unhappy with him.\n*As Harvison trained for his upcoming bout, he was annoyed that Cope was watching him. Harvison said in a confessional he knew \"a shady person\" when he saw one.\n*Someone wrote \"Chris Cope Double Agent\" in the sand in the zen garden. Cope asked his team whether they thought he was a spy or not, and most of the team said they had \"suspicions.\" Later that night, Cope entered the team bedroom and blamed Tony Ferguson for the message in the sand, based on handwriting similarities. Things got heated and everyone was paranoid.\n*Bowman confessed to a few of his teammates that he wrote the message, saying \"it was only meant to be a bit of fun.\" Ironically, his motive was that he sometimes thought that Cope was hanging around his team to bring information back to Team Lesnar.\n*'''Clay Harvison''' defeated Mick Bowman by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*During the fight, Harvison had blocked a switch kick with his hand, breaking his pinky finger with the bone sticking out of the skin. His TUF stint was in jeopardy.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23377/episode-no-5-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 5 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=April 27, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 6: Mean Streak'''\n*During a sparring session, Len Bentley collapsed in pain holding his knee. Lesnar was bothered by the injuries his team was sustaining.\n*Clay Harvison and Bentley were both at the hospital where they found that both their respective injuries were nothing too serious.\n*Dos Santos discovered that Lew Polley tried to visit the fighters' house on his own which made him feel like Polley was overstepping his boundaries once again. He then asked fellow coach, Luiz Dorea, for advice. Following Dorea's advice, dos Santos decides to send Polley home.\n*Lesnar announces the match-ups: Tony Ferguson vs. Justin Edwards and Chuck O'Neil vs. Zach Davis.\n*'''Tony Ferguson''' defeated Justin Edwards by KO (upkick) at 3:56 of the first round.\n*'''Zach Davis''' defeated Chuck O'Neil by submission (triangle choke) at 3:20 of the first round.\n*The wildcard match is decided. Edwards was picked, but was told that he cannot compete due to being suspended by the athletic commission.\n*The wildcard matches up Torres against O'Neil.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23481/episode-no-6-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|title=Episode No. 6 recap|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=May 5, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110507033054/http://mmajunkie.com/news/23481/episode-no-6-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-13-team-lesnar-vs-team-dos-santos.mma|archive-date=May 7, 2011}}\n\n;'''Episode 7: It's Just Good To Win'''\n*Len Bentley of Team Lesnar was confused about why he was not chosen for the wildcard match. He confronted White about it but instead, asked for a rematch with McGillivray at the finale.\n*Cope and Bailey got into a confrontation. Bailey stated that he was annoyed with Cope yelling \"woo\" and that he wanted to fight him.\n*Dos Santos beats Lesnar in the Coaches' Challenge which involved football drills.\n*After winning, dos Santos gave his share of the cash prize with members of the green team excluding Lesnar himself.\n*'''Chuck O'Neil''' defeated Javier Torres by submission (d'arce choke) at 4:31 in the second round.\n*The quarter-finals matches are announced:\n:*Clay Harvison vs Ramsey Nijem\n:*Chris Cope vs. Shamar Bailey\n:*Chuck O'Neil vs. Zach Davis\n:*Tony Ferguson vs. Ryan McGillivray\n\n;'''Episode 8: Shut Him Up'''\n*Shamar Bailey confided to his teammate about a back injury he had.\n*UFC middleweight [[Brian Stann]] arrived at the gym to give a pep-talk to the fighters.\n*'''Ramsey Nijem''' defeated Clay Harvison by submission (rear-naked choke) at :56 in the first round.\n*Cope asked his teammate Nordin Asrih for advice on fighting Bailey.\n*'''Chris Cope''' defeated Shamar Bailey by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 9: Then It Turned Ugly'''\n*Chuck O'Neil tried to share the $5000 he earned in his finish bonus with his friend Charlie Rader. It is revealed that Rader owes child support and had not seen his child in over a year.\n*'''Chuck O'Neil''' defeated Zach Davis via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*Davis returned to the house and reveals that he had suffered torn retinas in both his eyes. He said that the doctors have told him that he could no longer fight and that he would have to retire.\n*'''Tony Ferguson''' defeated Ryan McGillivray by TKO (punches) at :46 in the first round.\n*The semi-finals matches were announced:\n:*Chris Cope vs. Ramsey Nijem\n:*Tony Ferguson vs. Chuck O'Neil\n*Both teams joined to party after the fights. Rader poured alcohol on Ferguson as a joke, but his teammate reacted badly. Both got into a heated confrontation, which led to Ferguson mentioning Rader's problems involving his estranged son.\n*Members of both teams tried to break-up the fight. Ferguson left to cool off in the pool while the others comforted Rader inside, but when Ferguson came back, he started where he left off.\n*The argument was finally settled, but both teams were angered and disgusted by Ferguson's actions.\n\n;'''Episode 10: The Biggest Test'''\n*Tony Ferguson apologized to his team about his actions, but his team did not believe that his apology was sincere.\n*'''Ramsey Nijem''' defeated Chris Cope by TKO (punches) at 1:49 in the second round.\n*'''Tony Ferguson''' defeated Chuck O'Neil by TKO (punches) at 3:10 in the third round.\n*Nijem and Ferguson were announced as the season's finalists.\n*Following the conclusion of the episode, Lesnar phoned White to tell him that he had to pull out of his bout with dos Santos at UFC 131. White went on to say that Lesnar would be replaced by [[Shane Carwin]].\n\n==Tournament bracket==\n{{Round16\n\n||{{color box|#9AABA6|border=silver}} '''Shamar Bailey'''|'''UD'''|{{color Rampage vs. Hamill''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on May 28, 2011, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC130#/fight|title=UFC 130: Rampage vs. Hamill|publisher=UFC.com |date=2011-02-03}}\n\n==Background==\nThis event was to be the third meeting between [[Frankie Edgar]] and [[Gray Maynard]] and their second meeting for the [[UFC Lightweight Championship]]. The two met earlier in the year at [[UFC 125|UFC 125: Resolution]] with the fight ending in a split draw, and Edgar retaining his title. Their first encounter at [[UFC Fight Night: Florian vs. Lauzon]] saw Maynard win via unanimous decision.\n\nIt was later announced on May 9 that injuries to both forced the fight to be removed from the card. Edgar suffered broken ribs, while Maynard suffered a knee injury.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2011/05/09/frankie-edgar-vs-gray-maynard-off-ufc-130/|title=Frankie Edgar vs. Gray Maynard off UFC 130|work=MMAFighting.com|date=May 9, 2011 |last=Chiappetta |first=Mike |access-date=July 1, 2011}}\n\nOn February 9, it was rumored that [[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]] had been forced from his bout with [[Quinton \"Rampage\" Jackson]] and could be replaced by [[Rashad Evans]]. Silva stated that he was not injured and was looking forward to his bout with Jackson. Instead, information surfaced that Silva may have failed a pre-fight drug screening at [[UFC 125]], which could have resulted in his suspension.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22401/thiago-silvas-status-in-doubt-for-ufc-130-rampage-jackson-could-face-rashad-evans.mma |title=Thiago Silva's status in doubt for UFC 130, \"Rampage\" Jackson could face Rashad Evans |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |last1=Morgan |first1=John |last2=Marrocco |first2=Steven |date=February 9, 2011 |access-date=July 1, 2011 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110210031433/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22401/thiago-silvas-status-in-doubt-for-ufc-130-rampage-jackson-could-face-rashad-evans.mma |archive-date=February 10, 2011 }} It was then announced that the proposed Rashad Evans bout was never close to being signed, and that Rampage would face [[Matt Hamill]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22435/matt-hamill-replaces-thiago-silva-faces-rampage-jackson-at-ufc-130.mma|title=Matt Hamill replaces Thiago Silva, faces \"Rampage\" Jackson at UFC 130|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 11, 2011|access-date=February 11, 2011|archive-date=May 25, 2012|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525152647/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22435/matt-hamill-replaces-thiago-silva-faces-rampage-jackson-at-ufc-130.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nFormer [[Sengoku]] [[List of Sengoku champions#Middleweight Championship|Middleweight Champion]] [[Jorge Santiago]] confirmed he has signed a contract with the UFC and returned to face [[Brian Stann]] at this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/jorge-santiago-announces-hes-signed-with-the-ufc-faces-brian-stann-at-ufc-130|title=Jorge Santiago Announces UFC Signing, Faces Brian Stann at UFC 130|work=MMAWeekly.com |last=Martin |first=Damon |date=February 22, 2011 |access-date=July 1, 2011}}{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22444/brian-stann-likely-to-meet-jorge-santiago-not-wanderlei-silva-at-ufc-130.mma |title=Brian Stann likely to meet Jorge Santiago, not Wanderlei Silva, at UFC 130 |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=February 12, 2011 |last1=Marrocco |first1=Steven |last2=Stupp |first2=Dann |author-link2=Dann Stupp |access-date=July 1, 2011 |archive-date=July 14, 2011 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110714104427/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22444/brian-stann-likely-to-meet-jorge-santiago-not-wanderlei-silva-at-ufc-130.mma |url-status=dead }}\n\nOn April 20, [[Brad Pickett]] was forced to withdraw from his bout with [[Miguel Torres (fighter)|Miguel Torres]]. [[Demetrious Johnson]], who was set to fight [[Renan Barão]] on the undercard, stepped in as Pickett's replacement while Barão faced [[Cole Escovedo]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-131-fight-card-updates|title=UFC fight card updates|publisher=ufc.com|date=April 22, 2011}}\n\nOn April 27, [[Norifumi Yamamoto]] was forced out of his bout with [[Chris Cariaso]] due to injury. [[Michael McDonald (mixed martial artist)|Michael McDonald]], who was scheduled to take on [[Nick Pace]] at [[UFC 133]], stepped in as Yamamoto's replacement.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2011/4/28/2138565/ufc-130-kid-yamamoto-injured-michael-mcdonald-steps-in-against-chris|title=UFC 130: 'Kid' Yamamoto Injured, Michael McDonald Steps in Against Chris Cariaso|work=BloodyElbow.com |date=April 27, 2011 |first=Anton |last=Tabuena |access-date=July 1, 2011}}\n\n[[Cody McKenzie]] was set to fight [[Bart Palaszewski]] in this event, but an injury forced him out of the card. UFC president [[Dana White]] later announced that [[Gleison Tibau]] would step up to replace McKenzie.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-131-fight-card-updates|title=McKenzie out, Tibau In|publisher=ufc.com|date=May 5, 2011}} Palaszewski was also injured and replaced by returning UFC veteran [[Rafaello Oliveira]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Oliveira-Replaces-Palaszewski-Faces-Tibau-at-UFC-130-32483|title=Oliveira Replaces Palaszewski, Faces Tibau at UFC 130 |work=[[Sherdog.com]] |date=May 19, 2011 Morial Convention Center]] in [[New Orleans]], [[Louisiana]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFN25#/fight|title=UFC Fight Night: Shields vs. Ellenberger|date=14 September 2018 |publisher=ufc.com}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the seventh time that the organization has held an event in [[Louisiana]] and first since [[UFC 37]] in 2002.\n\nThe event was the last ''UFC Fight Night'' aired on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]], and in general, the last ''UFC Fight Night'' labeled event until the debut of [[Fox Sports 1]] in 2013. After that, all UFC main cards on cable/satellite Fox channels would be labeled ''UFC Fight Night''. And, in addition to cable/satellite Fox UFC events, most [[UFC Fight Pass]] main events would also be labeled ''UFC Fight Night''.\n\n[[Mackens Semerzier]] was expected to face Mike Lullo at the event,{{cite web |url=http://mmafrenzy.com/19130/mackens-semerzier-vs-mike-lullo-joins-ufc-fight-night-25-lineup/|title=Mackens Semerzier vs. Mike Lullo Joins UFC Fight Night 25 Lineup| publisher=mmafrenzy.com|date=July 15, 2011}} but pulled out of the event on August 31{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/25032/damarques-johnson-mackens-semerzier-scratched-from-ufc-fight-night-25.mma|title=Mackens Semerzier scratched from UFC Fight Night 23, 2011}} and was replaced by [[Robbie Peralta]]{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2011/9/5/240te781/ufc-fight-night-25-robert-peralta-vs-mike-lullo-booked-for-battle-on|title=Robert Peralta vs [[UFC 130|UFC 130: Rampage vs. Hamill]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 129: St-Pierre vs. Shields''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on Saturday, April 30, 2011, at [[Rogers Centre]] in [[Toronto]], [[Ontario]], Canada.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC129#/fight|title=UFC 129: St-Pierre vs. Shields|publisher=ufc.com|date=January 20, 2011}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the sixth that the UFC has hosted in [[Canada]] and the first sanctioned mixed martial arts event in [[Ontario]] since the sport was legalized in the province in August 2010.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2011/03/30/ontarios-first-sanctioned-mma-event-set-for-saturday/ |title=Ontario's First Sanctioned MMA Event Set for Saturday |publisher=mmafighting.com|date=March 30, 2011}}\nThe Toronto event was originally titled UFC 131 by the promotion, but later changed to UFC 129 as its early 2011 schedule started to fall more firmly into place.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufcs-toronto-debut-re-dubbed-ufc-129-2011-schedule-falling-in-place |title=UFC's Toronto Debut Re-Dubbed UFC 129; 2011 Schedule Falling In Place |work=MMAWeekly.com|date=December 16, 2010 |last=Pishna |first=Ken |access-date=July 1, 2011}}\n[[Image:UFC 129.jpg|thumb|left|250px|A crowd of 55,000 filled [[Rogers Centre]] for UFC 129.]]\n\nIt was the largest UFC event in North American history{{cite web|url=http://www.fightline.com/fl/news/2010/1207/534499/dana-white/|title=UFC 131 And Fan Expo Weekend Announced For Toronto In April|work=Fightline.com|date=December 7, 2010|first=Raj|last=Giri|access-date=July 1, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110711001558/http://www.fightline.com/fl/news/2010/1207/534499/dana-white/|archive-date=July 11, 2011}}{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-129-st-pierre-vs-shields-set-for-42000-fans-at-rogers-centre-in-toronto|title=UFC 129: St-Pierre vs. Shields Set For 42,000 Fans at Rogers Centre in Toronto |publisher=mmaweekly.com|date=2011-01-26}} and coincided with a two-day UFC Fan Expo at the [[Direct Energy Centre]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Toronto-to-Host-First-Ever-Ontario-Event-on-Apr-30 |title=Toronto To Host First-Ever Ontario Event on April 30 |publisher=ufc.com|date=December 7, 2010}}{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21653/ufc-131-official-for-april-30-at-rogers-centre-in-toronto.mma |title=UFC 131 official for April 30 at Rogers Centre in Toronto |publisher=mmajunkie.com |date=December 7, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120711115510/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21653/ufc-131-official-for-april-30-at-rogers-centre-in-toronto.mma |archive-date=July 11, 2012 }} UFC officials had originally planned for the Rogers Centre to be configured to hold 42,000 fans for UFC 129. The UFC put those tickets on sale to UFC Fight Club members in a special pre-sale on February 10, 2011, selling nearly all of them. They released and sold more tickets the following day to UFC newsletter subscribers in a second special pre-sale. UFC 129 then sold out when tickets went on sale to the general public on February 12, with officials scrambling to up the number of seats to 55,000.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-129-sold-out-shatters-records-with-55000-tickets |title=UFC 129 Sold Out, Shatters Records With 55,000 Tickets |publisher=MMAWeekly.com|date=February 12, 2011}} Selling out all 55,000 tickets for gate revenues exceeding $11 million,{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-129-gate-receipts-total-11-million-eclipse-record |title=UFC 129 Gate Receipts Total $11 Million, Eclipse Record |work=MMAWeekly.com |last=Pishna |first=Ken |date=February 15, 2011 |access-date=July 1, 2011}} the event shatters both MMA attendance and gate records in North America.\n\n[[John Makdessi]] was rumored to face [[Jonathan Brookins]] at the event; however, Makdessi instead fought [[Kyle Watson (fighter)|Kyle Watson]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22055/john-makdessi-meets-kyle-watson-not-jonathan-brookins-at-ufc-129.mma |title=John Makdessi meets Kyle Watson, not Jonathan Brookins, at UFC 129 |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=January 11, 2011 |last=Marrocco |first=Steven |access-date=July 1, 2011 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120716184832/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22055/john-makdessi-meets-kyle-watson-not-jonathan-brookins-at-ufc-129.mma |archive-date=July 16, 2012 }}\n\nIn a conference call for ''[[UFC: Fight For The Troops 2]]'' in January 2011, [[Dana White]] confirmed that [[List of UFC champions#Featherweight Championship|UFC Featherweight Champion]] [[José Aldo]] would defend his title against [[Mark Hominick]] on this card, provided that Hominick defeated [[George Roop]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22059/hominick-gets-featherweight-title-shot-with-win-over-roop-at-ufc-fight-night-23.mma |title=Hominick gets Featherweight Title shot with win over Roop at UFC Fight Night 23 |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=January 11, 2011 |last=Marrocco |first=Steven |access-date=July 1, 2011 |archive-date=March 3, 2011 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110303233340/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22059/hominick-gets-featherweight-title-shot-with-win-over-roop-at-ufc-fight-night-23.mma |url-status=dead }} White also stated that, regardless of the outcome of the Hominick/Roop fight, Aldo would still defend his Featherweight Championship at this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22160/jose-aldo-official-for-ufc-129-slot-four-additional-contests-set-for-toronto.mma|title=Jose Aldo official for UFC 129 slot; four additional contests set for Toronto|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=January 20, 2010|access-date=January 20, 2011|archive-date=January 24, 2011|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110124005709/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22160/jose-aldo-official-for-ufc-129-slot-four-additional-contests-set-for-toronto.mma|url-status=dead}} Hominick defeated Roop, via first-round TKO, to confirm his place as no. 1 contender, and solidify the bout with Aldo. After the bout, judge Doug Crosby made a mistake scoring the 5th round 10–8 in favor of José Aldo instead of Mark Hominick, making the final tally 50–43 for Aldo. A correction was made after the bout, and the correct score was 48–45 in favor of Aldo.{{cite web martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on June 11, 2011, at the [[Rogers Arena]] in [[Vancouver|Vancouver, British Columbia]], [[Canada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC131#/fight|title= UFC 131: DOS SANTOS VS. CARWIN|publisher=UFC.com|access-date=2011-04-05}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Image:UFC 131 Carwin vs. JDS 3.jpg|left|thumb|200px|Junior dos Santos fought Shane Carwin in the main event of ''UFC 131''.]]\n[[Brock Lesnar]] was originally scheduled to face [[Junior dos Santos]] after their coaching stint on ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 13]]'', but due to a recurring illness, Lesnar was replaced by [[Shane Carwin]].{{cite news|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=6535275 |title=Diverticulitis forces Brock Lesnar out |first=Franklin |last=McNeil |work=[[ESPN.com]] |date=May 12, 2011 |access-date=May 12, 2011}} Originally, a matchup between Carwin,{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaentry.com/latest-recent-mma-news/daily-digest/ufc-131-shane-carwin-in-call-it-a-comeback.html |title=UFC 131: Shane Carwin in – Call it a Comeback? |work=MMAentry.com |date=June 29, 2011 |last=Samuels |first=Albert |access-date=June 29, 2011 }}{{Dead link|date=July 2018 |bot=InternetArchiveBot |fix-attempted=no }} and [[Jon Olav Einemo]] was scheduled for this event. Einemo remained on the card and faced [[Dave Herman (fighter)|Dave Herman]], who was pulled from a matchup with [[Joey Beltran]] as a result. Beltran was matched up with [[Aaron Rosa]].\n\n[[Court McGee]] was scheduled to fight Jesse Bongfeldt, but was forced to pull out due to a torn [[Medial collateral ligament|MCL]] while training and was replaced by [[Chris Weidman]]{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-131-fight-card-updates|title=McGee out, Weidman in at UFC 131|publisher=UFC.com|date=April 5, 2011}}{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/court-mcgee-suffers-torn-mcl-drops-out-of-ufc-131-fight-against-bongfeldt|title=Court McGee suffers torn MCL drops out of UFC 131|publisher=mmaweekly.com|date=April 4, 2011 |last=Martin |first=Damon |access-date=June 10, 2011}}\n\n[[Rani Yahya]] was scheduled face [[Dustin Poirier]] at this event, but withdrew due to injury on 3 May 2011 and was replaced by UFC newcomer [[Jason Young (fighter)|Jason Young]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23476/with-yahya-injured-poirier-now-meets-newcomer-young-at-ufc-131.mma|title=With Yahya Injured, Poirier now meets newcomer Young at UFC 131|publisher=MMAJunkie|date=May 3, 2012}}\n\n[[Anthony Perosh]] was expected to face [[Krzysztof Soszynski]] at this event, but Perosh was forced from the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Igor Pokrajac]].{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-131-news-anthony-perosh-out-krzysztof-soszynski-now-draws-igor-pokrajac|title=UFC 131 News: Anthony Perosh Out, Krzysztof Soszynski Now Draws Igor Pokrajac|work=mmaweekly.com|date=May 11, 2011 |last=Pishna |first=Ken |access-date=June 10, 2011}} However, Pokrajac was forced to pull out of the bout due to injuries just days before the event and was replaced by [[Mike Massenzio]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-131-fight-card-updates|title= Massenzio Tapped for Soszynski Bout on Saturday|publisher=UFC.com|date=June 7, 2011}} The Soszynski/Pokrajac bout was later rescheduled for [[UFC 140]], where Pokrajac won by knockout.\n\nOn 17 May a torn muscle forced [[Mac Danzig]] out of his bout with [[Donald Cerrone]].{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/mac-danzig-injured-out-of-ufc-131-fight-against-donald-cowboy-cerrone|title=Mac Danzig Injured; Out of UFC 131 Fight Against Donald 'Cowboy' Cerrone|publisher=mmaweekly.com|date=May 17, 2011 |last=Martin |first=Damon |access-date=June 10, 2011}} Promotional newcomer [[Vagner Rocha]] replaced Danzig.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/23641/mac-danzig-out-at-ufc-131-vagner-rocha-now-faces-donald-cerrone.mma|title=Mac Danzig out at UFC 131, Vagner Rocha now faces Donald Cerrone|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=May 17, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120716154919/http://mmajunkie.com/news/23641/mac-danzig-out-at-ufc-131-vagner-rocha-now-faces-donald-cerrone.mma|archive-date=July 16, 2012}}\n\nUFC newcomer [[Dave Herman (fighter)|Dave Herman]] was originally slated to face [[Rob Broughton]] at this event, but Broughton was later replaced by [[Joey Beltran]], who was later replaced 132|UFC 132: Cruz vs. Faber]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC Live: Hardy vs. Lytle]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 133: Evans vs. Ortiz 2''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on August 6, 2011, at the [[Wells Fargo Center (Philadelphia)|Wells Fargo Center]] in [[Philadelphia]], [[Pennsylvania]], [[United States]].{{cite web |url=https://www.ufc.com/event/UFC133#/fight|title=UFC 133: Evans vs. Ortiz|publisher=UFC.com|date=May 17, 2011}}{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2011/02/02/philadelphia-targeted-for-ufc-133/ |title=Philadelphia Targeted for UFC 133|work=MMAfighting.com|date=February 2, 2011 |last=Chiappetta |first=Mike |access-date=July 1, 2011}}\n\n==Background==\nMultiple fighters that were expected to fight at this event suffered injuries. This led to multiple fights, including championship bouts being called off as a result.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/780787-ufc-133-and-the-5-most-injury-plagued-cards-in-ufc-history#/articles/780787-ufc-133-and-the-5-most-injury-plagued-cards-in-ufc-history |title=UFC 133 and the five most injury plagued cards in UFC history|work=bleacherreport.com|date=July 26, 2011 |last=Dreisbach |first=Blake |access-date=July 26, 2011}}\n\nThis event was expected to feature [[List of UFC champions#Light Heavyweight Championship|Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Jon Jones]]' first title defense against former Light Heavyweight Champion [[Rashad Evans]], but a pre-existing injury in his right hand prevented Jones from fighting, and was expected to keep him sidelined until late 2011.{{cite web |url=https://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-133-fight-card-updates |title=UFC 133 fight card updates|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=2011-04-21}} Not willing to wait, Evans accepted a fight against [[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]] at this event. However, Jones later found out that he would no longer require surgery and instead made his first title defense against [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] on September 24, 2011, at [[UFC 135]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24004/as-expected-champ-jones-and-challenger-rampage-headline-ufc-135.mma|title=. As expected, champ Jon Jones and challenger \"Rampage\" Jackson headline UFC 135|work=[[MMAjunkie.com]]|date=June 15, 2011|last=Stupp|first=Dann|author-link=Dann Stupp|access-date=July 1, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110618020357/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24004/as-expected-champ-jones-and-challenger-rampage-headline-ufc-135.mma|archive-date=June 18, 2011}}\n\nOn July 12, [[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]] pulled out of his main event bout with [[Rashad Evans]] due to a knee injury suffered during training.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2011/07/12/sources-injury-forces-phil-davis-out-of-ufc-133-main-event/|title=Sources: Injury Forces Phil Davis Out of UFC 133 Main Event|publisher=MMAFighting|date=July 12, 2011 |last=Helwani |first=Ariel |access-date=July 12, 2011}} [[Tito Ortiz]] initially declined an offer to step in as Davis' replacement{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2011/07/12/tito-ortiz-turns-down-opportunity-for-rematch-with-rashad-evans/|title=Tito Ortiz Turns Down Opportunity for Rematch With Rashad Evans|publisher=MMAFighting|date=July 12, 2011 |last=Chiapetta |first=Mike |access-date=July 12, 2011}} and Evans was then rumored to face [[Lyoto Machida]] in a rematch of their title bout at [[UFC 98]].{{cite web|url=http://www.headkicklegend.com/2011/7/12/2273117/ufc-133-fight-card-phil-davis-out-rashad-evans-set-to-rematch-lyoto|title=UFC 133 Fight Card: Phil Davis Out. Rashad Evans Set to Rematch Lyoto Machida |publisher=headkicklegend.com|date=July 12, 2011 |last=Roth |first=Matthew |access-date=July 12, 2011}}{{cite web |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2011/7/13/2273833/ufc-133-fight-card-lyoto-machida-vs-rashad-evans-official |title=UFC 133 Fight Card: Lyoto Machida vs. Rashad Evans a Done Deal |work=bloodyelbow.com |last=Brookhouse |first=Brent |date=July 13, 2011 |access-date=July 13, 2011 |archive-date=September 19, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120919071909/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2011/7/13/2273833/ufc-133-fight-card-lyoto-machida-vs-rashad-evans-official |url-status=dead }} Machida's camp verbally accepted the fight, but when UFC President [[Dana White]] called Machida's camp again to confirm the fight, they said Machida would fight only if he received higher pay, which White declined.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24382/white-machida-reneged-on-ufc-133-committment-ortizs-job-no-longer-on-line.mma |title=White: Machida reneged on UFC 133 {{sic|nolink=y|commi|ttment|expected=commitment}} |work=mmajunkie.com |last=Morgan |first=John |date=July 13, 2011 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110715202136/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24382/white-machida-reneged-on-ufc-133-committment-ortizs-job-no-longer-on-line.mma |archive-date=July 15, 2011 }} The organization briefly turned their attention to matching Evans with [[Vladimir Matyushenko]], who was already scheduled to compete on the card.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2011/07/dana-whites-plan-d-for-rashad-evans-at-ufc-133-vladimir-matyushenko/|title=Dana White's Plan D for Rashad Evans at UFC 133? Vladimir Matyushenko |work=mmajunkie.com |author= |date=July 14, 2011}} In the meanwhile, Ortiz had a change of heart and accepted the offer to replace Davis. The two previously met at [[UFC 73]] where the bout ended in a Draw.{{cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-133-evans-vs-tito-2-announcement|title=Davis Injured, Ortiz Gets Call To Face Evans in UFC 133 Rematch |work=ufc.com |date=July 13, 2011}} The Evans/Davis bout was later rescheduled for [[UFC on Fox 2]] on January 28, 2012.\n\n[[Nick Pace]] was originally slated to take on [[Michael McDonald (mixed martial artist)|Michael McDonald]] at this event, before McDonald was pulled to replace [[Norifumi Yamamoto]] for a [[UFC 130]] bout with [[Chris Cariaso]].{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2011/4/28/2138565/ufc-130-kid-yamamoto-injured-michael-mcdonald-steps-in-against-chris|title=UFC 130: 'Kid' Yamamoto Injured, Michael McDonald Steps in Against Chris Cariaso|work=BloodyElbow.com|date=April 27, 2011 |first=Anton |last=Tabuena |access-date=July 1, 2011}} [[Ivan Menjivar]] would later step in to face Pace at this event. At the official weigh ins on August 5, Menjivar could not make the 136 pound weight allowance cut off, and Pace subsequently agreed to fight Menjivar in a 138-pound catchweight bout.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/ufc-133-evans-vs-ortiz-official-weigh-in-results-from-philadelphia|title=UFC 133: Evans vs. Ortiz Official Weigh-in Results (UPDATED)|work=MMAweekly.com|date=August 5, 2011 |access-date=August 5, 2011}}\n\nA bout between [[List of UFC champions#Featherweight Championship|Featherweight Champion]] [[José Aldo]] and [[Chad Mendes]] was previously linked to this event.{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/jose-aldo-vs-chad-mendes-possible-for-ufc-133-in-philadelphia|title=José Aldo vs Chad Mendes possible for UFC 133 in Philadelphia|work=MMAWeekly.com|date=May 1, 2011 |last=Fontanez |first=Erik |access-date=July 1, 2011}} However, Aldo was sidelined until October recovering from various injuries, while Mendes remained on the card and faced [[Rani Yahya]].{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Aldo-out-Until-Fall-Mendes-Meets-Yahya-at-UFC-133-in-Philly-32589|title=Aldo out Until Fall; Mendes Meets Yahya at UFC 133 in Philly|work=[[Sherdog.com]] |date=May 24, 2011 |last=Whitman |first=Mike |access-date=July 1, 2011}} The Aldo/Mendes title fight would later take place at [[UFC 142]] on January 14, 2012.\n\n[[Riki Fukuda]] was scheduled{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaentry.com/upcoming-mma-ufc/fight-card-event-details-results/ufc-133-evans-vs-davis.html |title=UFC 133: Fight Card Lineup |work=MMAentry.com |date=June 29, 2011 |last=Samuels |first=Albert |access-date=July 5, 2011 }}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} to face [[Rafael Natal]], but was forced out of the bout due to a knee injury sustained in a car accident{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24058/riki-fukuda-out-of-ufc-133-due-to-car-accident-injuries.mma|title=Riki Fukuda out of UFC 133 due to car-accident was replaced by [[Costas Philippou]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24063/replacement-costantinos-phillippou-meets-rafael-sapo-natal-at-ufc-133.mma|title=Replacement Constantinos Philippou '''''UFC on Versus 5''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on August 14, 2011, at the [[Bradley Center]] in [[Milwaukee]], [[Wisconsin]].{{cite web|title=UFC Live: Hardy vs. Lytle|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UVS5|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=2011-07-15}} The event was the first that the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] has hosted in [[Wisconsin]]. The event was broadcast on [[Rogers Sportsnet]] and [[Versus (TV channel)|Versus]].\n\n==Background==\n[[Karlos Vemola]] was originally scheduled to face [[Stephan Bonnar]] in this event, but Bonnar was replaced by newcomer [[Ronny Markes]] after sustaining a training injury.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/uvs5-fight-card-updates|title=Bonnar injured, replaced by newcomer Markes|publisher=ufc.com|date=June 17, 2011}}\n\n[[Paul Taylor (fighter)|Paul Taylor]] was scheduled to face [[John Makdessi]] at this event, but Makdessi was forced from the bout with an injury{{cite was replaced by [[Donald Cerrone]].{{cite web|url=http://www.espn.co.uk/ufc/sport/story/98453.html|title=Paul Taylor gets Cerrone examination on August 14\n|publisher=espn.co.uk.|date=June 27, 2011}} (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 24''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on March 26, 2011, at the [[KeyArena]] in [[Seattle]], [[Washington (state)|Washington]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFN24|title=UFC Fight Night: Seattle|publisher=ufc.com|date=January 13, 2011}}\n\n==Background==\n\nThe event did not lead into the season premiere of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 13]]'' as has been the case for most of that program's seasonal debuts. That took place on March 30.\n\nOn February 19, 2011, [[Dana White]] alerted followers on [[Twitter]] that [[Tito Ortiz]] had to pull out of his fight with [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]], after receiving a severe cut on his forehead, and would be replaced by [[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22541/tito-ortiz-out-phil-davis-faces-antonio-rogerio-nogueira-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|title=Tito Ortiz out, Phil Davis faces Antonio Rogerio Nogueira at UFC Fight Night 24|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 19, 2011|access-date=February 19, 2011|archive-date=February 20, 2011|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110220221028/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22541/tito-ortiz-out-phil-davis-faces-antonio-rogerio-nogueira-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nOn March 5, [[Duane Ludwig]] was forced out of his fight with [[Amir Sadollah]] due to injury. [[James Wilks]] was tapped as his replacement.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufn-24-wilks-replaces-ludwig|title=TUF Winners Collide on March 26|publisher=UFC.com|date=March 5, 2011}} Wilks then injured himself, and was forced to withdraw from the bout. [[DaMarques Johnson]] was the replacement.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2011/3/13/2048280/ufc-fight-night-24-amir-sadollah-vs-damarques-johnson-booked-with|title=UFC Fight Night 24: Amir Sadollah vs DaMarques Johnson booked with James Wilks out|work=MMAMania.com|date=March 13, 2011 |first=Geno |last=Mrosko |access-date=July 5, 2011 }}\nAlso on March 5, [[Dennis Hallman]] suffered a knee injury in training and was forced out of his bout against [[TJ Waldburger]]. [[Johny Hendricks]] stepped in as Hallman's replacement.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22727/hendricks-replaces-hallman-faces-waldburger-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|title=Hallman out, Hendricks in against Waldburger|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=March 5, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110307155326/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22727/hendricks-replaces-hallman-faces-waldburger-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|archive-date=March 7, 2011}}\n\nOn March 16, [[Nick Pace]] was forced out of his bout against [[Michael McDonald (mixed martial artist)|Michael McDonald]] due to suffering an injury. Promotional newcomer [[Edwin Figueroa]] stepped in as his replacement.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22857/newcomer-edwin-figueroa-in-nick-pace-out-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|title=Pace out, Figueroa in|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=March 5, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110322021316/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22857/newcomer-edwin-figueroa-in-nick-pace-out-at-ufc-fight-night-24.mma|archive-date=March 22, 2011}}\n\nAlso on March 16, a broken foot forced [[Nam Phan]] out of his rematch with [[Leonard Garcia]] and was replaced by [[Chan Sung Jung]] creating a rematch from their Fight of the Night performance at [[WEC 48|WEC 48: Aldo vs. Faber]], which ended in a controversial decision win for Garcia (29–28, 28–29, 29–28).\n\nThe [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] announced via their official [[Twitter]] account that five preliminary bouts would stream on [[Facebook]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22969/three-new-bouts-added-to-ufc-fight-night-24s-free-facebook-stream.mma|title=Three new bouts added to UFC Fight Night 24's free Facebook stream|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=March 24, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110819110802/http://mmajunkie.com/news/22969/three-new-bouts-added-to-ufc-fight-night-24s-free-facebook-stream.mma|archive-date=August 19, 2011}} This would also be the last UFC event until [[UFC on Fox: Evans vs. Davis]] the following year to feature at least one unaired preliminary fight.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110630183252/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24162/ufc-on-versus-4-earns-reported-xx-attendance-figure-for-xxk-gate.mma |archive-date=June 30, '''''UFC on Versus 4''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on June 26, 2011, at [[Consol Energy Center]] in [[Pittsburgh]], [[Pennsylvania]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFCLive4#/fight|title=UFC Live: Kongo vs. Barry|publisher=ufc.com|date=April 6, 2011}}\nThe event was broadcast on [[Versus (TV channel)|Versus]] in the U.S. and was broadcast on [[Rogers Sportsnet]] in Canada.\n\n==Background==\nThis is the fourth live UFC card to appear on Versus.\n\n[[Matt Riddle|Matthew Riddle]] was expected to face [[T. J. Grant]], but was forced from the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Charlie Brenneman]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/tuf-13-finale-uvs4-fight-card-updates|title=Fight card updates|publisher=ufc.com}} However, just days before the event Grant was forced from the card due to an unknown illness. Without proper time to find a replacement the bout was scrapped.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24091/t-j-grant-withdraws-from-ufc-on-versus-4-fight-with-charlie-brenneman-scrapped.mma|title=T.J. Grant withdraws from UFC on Versus 4, fight with Charlie Brenneman scrapped|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=2011-06-22|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110627163643/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24091/t-j-grant-withdraws-from-ufc-on-versus-4-fight-with-charlie-brenneman-scrapped.mma|archive-date=2011-06-27}}\n\n[[Anthony Johnson (fighter)|Anthony Johnson]] was expected to face [[Nate Marquardt]] in the main event, but was forced from the bout with a rotator cuff injury and replaced by [[Rick Story]]. However, Nate Marquardt did not receive medical clearance the day of the weigh-ins and therefore pulled out of the main event. Charlie Brenneman, whose earlier bout with T. J. Grant was removed from the card, stepped in to fight [[Rick Story]], while the [[Pat Barry (kickboxer)|Pat Barry]] vs. [[Cheick Kongo]] fight was promoted to the main event.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24140/marquardt-scratched-from-ufc-on-versus-4-barry-kongo-now-main-story-gets-brenneman.mma |title=Marquardt scratched from UFC on Versus 4; Barry-Kongo now main, Story gets Brenneman |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=June 25, 2011 |last1=Morgan |first1=John |last2=Stupp |first2=Dann |access-date=June 27, 2011 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110628201756/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24140/marquardt-scratched-from-ufc-on-versus-4-barry-kongo-now-main-story-gets-brenneman.mma |archive-date=June 28, 2011 }} Dana White subsequently announced that Marquardt would be cut from the UFC due to the seriousness of the failed medical clearance but because of Pennsylvania health privacy laws, White could not disclose the specifics of the case {{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24163/all-parties-still-silent-on-nate-marquardts-failed-medical-clearance.mma|title=Dana White says Nate Marqaurdt's UFC days are done following UFC on Versus 4 scratch|work=[[MMAjunkie.com]]|date=June 25, 2011|last=Stupp|first=Dann|access-date=June 27, 2011|archive-date=June 29, 2011|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110629162336/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24163/all-parties-still-silent-on-nate-marquardts-failed-medical-clearance.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Martin Kampmann]] was expected to face [[John Howard (fighter)|John Howard]], but was forced from the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]]. Brown's original opponent [[Rich Attonito]] faced [[Daniel Roberts (fighter)|Daniel Roberts]].\n\nThe entire preliminary card was streamed on [[Facebook]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/24066/ufc-on-versus-4-preliminary-card-to-stream-on-facebook.mma|title=UFC on Versus 4 preliminary card to stream on Facebook preliminary-card bouts|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=June 20, 2011|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110625015257/http://mmajunkie.com/news/24066/ufc-on-versus-4-preliminary-card-to-stream-on-facebook.mma|archive-date=June 25, 2011}}\n\nThe event drew an estimated 744,000 viewers on Versus.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Versus-Broadcast-of-UFC-Live-4-Nets-744000-Viewers-33531|title=Versus Broadcast of UFC Live 4 Nets 744,000 Viewers|publisher=sherdog.com|date=June 28, 2011}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Versus)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Cheick [[Dana White]]\n|starring = [[Dana White]], [[Michael Bisping]], [[Jason Miller (fighter)|Jason Miller]]\n|country = United States\n|network = [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]]\n|first_aired = {{start 2018}}\n|last_aired =\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Bisping vs. Team Miller{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2011/7/7/2264305/the-ultimate-fighter-tuf-14-video-teaser-for-team-bisping-vs-team|title=The Ultimate Fighter (TUF) 14 video teaser for 'Team Bisping vs Team Miller'|website=Mmamania.com|access-date=26 January 2018}}''''' is the fourteenth installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced reality television series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.\n\nThe UFC and Spike held open tryouts on March 21, 2011, in Newark, New Jersey. The casting call went out for [[Bantamweight (MMA)|Bantamweight]] and [[Featherweight (MMA)|Featherweight]] fighters. All fighters that applied and tried out for the show must have had at least three professional fights and have been at least 21 years of age. Some of the fighters that showed up for the tryouts included UFC veterans [[Din Thomas]] & [[Kit Cope]], future UFC fighter [[Ian McCall (fighter)|Ian McCall]], WEC veteran [[Wagnney Fabiano]], and future [[Bellator Fighting Championship|Bellator]] tournament finalist [[Alexis Vila]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/22921/tuf-14-tryouts-draw-notables-din-thomas-wagnney-fabiano-micah-miller-kit-cope.mma|title=\"TUF 14\" tryouts draw notables Din Thomas, Wagnney Fabiano, Micah Miller, Kit Cope|publisher=Mmajunkie.com|date=March 21, 2011}} Rumors had surfaced of [[Chael Sonnen]] and [[Michael Bisping]] being possible coaches, though [[Jason Miller (fighter)|Jason Miller]] offered via Twitter to replace Sonnen if he is unable.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmabay.co.uk/2011/04/06/tuf-14-jason-%E2%80%9Cmayhem%E2%80%9D-miller-offers-to-step-in-for-chael-sonnen-and-stir-the-pot-opposite-michael-bisping|title=TUF 14 – Jason \"Mayhem\" Miller offers to step in for Chael Sonnen and stir the pot opposite Michael Bisping|publisher=Mmabay.co.uk|date=April 6, 2011}} Miller and Bisping were later confirmed as coaches by Dana White.{{cite web|url=http://www.mixedmartialarts.com/news/328765/Dana-White-Bisping-Mayhem-to-coach-TUF-14|title=Mixed Martial Arts: MMA News, Fighters, Events, Forum|website=Mixedmartialarts.com|access-date=26 January 2018}}\n\n==Cast==\n===Coaches===\n;{{color box|lightblue|border=silver}} {{color box|blue|border=silver}} Team Bisping:\n*[[Michael Bisping]]\n*Brady Fink\n*[[Tiki Ghosn]]\n*[[Rob Dillashaw]], [[John Albert (fighter)|John Albert]], Josh Ferguson.\n** '''Featherweights''': [[Diego Brandão]], [[Akira Corassani]], [[Marcus Brimage]], Stephen Bass.\n* '''Team Miller'''\n** '''Bantamweights''': [[John Dodson (fighter)|John Dodson]], [[Johnny Bedford]], [[Dustin Pague]], [[Roland Delorme]].\n** '''Featherweights''': [[Dennis Bermudez]], [[Bryan Caraway]], [[Dustin Neace]], [[Steven Siler]].\n*'''Fighters eliminated before entry round'''\n** '''Bantamweights''': Carson Beebe, Casey Dyer, B.J. Ferguson, Tateki Matsuda, Matt Jaggers, Paul McVeigh, Brandon Merkt, Orville Smith\n** '''Featherweights''': Josh Clopton, Karsten Lenjoint, Eric Marriott, Micah Miller, [[Jimmie Rivera]], Jesse Newell, Brian Pearman, Bryson Wailehua-Hansen\n\n==Episodes==\n'''Episode 1: Scrappers'''\n*[[Dana White]] welcomed the 32 fighters, consisting of 16 featherweights and 16 bantamweights, to the show at the [[Mandalay Bay Events Center]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]]. He introduced them to the coaches, middleweights [[Jason Miller (fighter)|Jason \"Mayhem\" Miller]] and [[Michael Bisping|Michael \"The Count\" Bisping]].\n*White dismissed the fighters to pre-fight warm-ups for the elimination round.\n*The preliminary fights began:\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''Josh Ferguson''' defeated Casey Dyer via knock out (punches) at 0:14 of round 1.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Diego Brandão''' defeated Jesse Newell via knock out (punches) at 0:47 of round 1.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''John Dodson''' defeated Brandon Merkt via technical knock out (punches) at 1:37 of round 1.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Dennis Bermudez''' defeated Jimmie Rivera via technical knock out (punches) at 1:40 of round 2.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''Roland Delorme''' defeated B.J. Ferguson via submission (triangle choke) at 1:56 of round 1.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Marcus Brimage''' defeated Bryson Wailehua-Hansen via technical knock out (strikes) at 0:21 of round 2.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''Johnny Bedford''' defeated Carson Beebe via submission (neck crank/guillotine choke) at 4:19 of round 1.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''Dustin Pague''' defeated Tateki Matsuda via majority decision (19-19, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''Louis Gaudinot''' defeated Paul McVeigh via technical knock out (strikes) at 4:59 of round 3.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Bryan Caraway''' defeated Eric Marriott via unanimous decision (20-18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Dustin Neace''' defeated Josh Clopton via unanimous decision (20-18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''TJ Dillashaw''' defeated Matt Jaggers via technical knock out (strikes) at 5:00 of round 1.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Steven Siler''' defeated Micah Miller via submission (guillotine choke) at 0:53 of round 3.\n:*Bantamweight bout: '''John Albert''' defeated Orville Smith via submission (rear naked choke) in round 1.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Stephen Bass''' defeated Karsten Lenjoint via submission (triangle choke) in round 2.\n:*Featherweight bout: '''Akira Corassani''' defeated Brian Pearman via knock out (punches) at 4:17 of round 1.\n*White was impressed with the elimination round fights and congratulated the winning fighters on making it onto the show.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/25327/episode-no-1-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-14-team-bisping-vs-team-miller.mma|title=Episode No. 1 recap|date=September 21, 2011|author=Dann Stupp|website=Mmajunkie.com|access-date=26 January 2018|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20111025001836/http://mmajunkie.com/news/25327/episode-no-1-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-14-team-bisping-vs-team-miller.mma|archive-date=October 25, 2011|df=mdy-all}}\n\n'''Episode 2: I Against I'''\n\n*The fighters moved into the house, picked their beds and had a barbecue.\n*Bisping and Miller selected their teams and White flipped a coin (blue for Bisping, orange for Miller).\n*Bisping won and chose the first pick of the fighters, so Miller got to pick the first fight.\n*'''Bantamweights:'''\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n|-\n!Bisping\n|Louis Gaudinot\n|T.J. Dillashaw\n|John Albert\n|Josh Ferguson\n|-\n!Miller\n|John Dodson\n|Johnny Bedford\n|Dustin Pague\n|Roland Delorme\n|}\n\n*'''Featherweights:'''\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n|-\n!Bisping\n|Diego Brandão\n|Akira Corassani\n|Marcus Brimage\n|Stephen Bass\n|-\n!Miller\n|Dennis Bermudez\n|Bryan Caraway\n|Dustin Neace\n|Steven Siler\n|}\n*Miller announced the first featherweight fight and matched Caraway against Brimage.\n*Miller visited his team at the house and brought ''Normatec MVP Pro'' compression suits to help them recover faster after training.\n*Miller pranked Bisping by taking the training tires and putting them in the blue team's dressing room.\n*Brimage got help from Bisping and his team with cutting weight, while Caraway dealt with severe anxiety issues about the fight.\n*'''Bryan Caraway''' defeated Marcus Brimage via submission (rear-naked choke) at 2:55 of round 2.\n\n'''Episode 3: Death Leprechauns'''\n\n*Bisping got Miller back after his tire prank in the dressing room by jacking the tires on Mayhem's car.\n*Tempers flared during a Team Bisping sparring session, especially between Diego Brandão and Marcus Brimage.\n*The first bantamweight matchup was announced: Johnny Bedford vs. Josh Ferguson.\n*Knowing he was going to be picked by Team Miller, Ferguson dedicated a note of profanity he wrote just for Bedford.\n*After Akira Corassani's pranks increased, a bitter rivalry began between him and Dustin Neace.\n*Missing his friends on Bisping's team, John Dodson made up his own team with Louis Gaudinot and Ferguson called the \"Death Leprechauns\", a team of fighters who fight naturally at flyweight.\n*Team Bisping was made aware of Bedford's injured right hand by Dodson of Team Miller, who was trying to help Ferguson and is a mole for Bisping's team.\n*'''Johnny Bedford''' defeated Josh Ferguson via unanimous decision after 2 rounds.\n\n'''Episode 4: Get the F Up'''\n\n*Bisping did not show up for the featherweight fight announcement and his team wonders where he has been.\n*The featherweight matchup was announced: Dennis Bermudez vs. Stephen Bass.\n*'''Dennis Bermudez''' defeated Stephen Bass via technical knock out (referee stoppage due to punches) at 2:58 of round 2.\n*Bass blamed Bisping for his loss, due to overworking him in practice the day before.\n*The bantamweight matchup was announced: Dustin Pague vs. Louis Gaudinot.\n*Bisping used insider information from Dodson to prepare his next fighter.\n*'''Dustin Pague''' defeated Louis Gaudinot via submission (rear-naked choke) at 2:32 of round 2.\n*Miller was happy that his team was now 4–0 over Bisping's team.\n\n'''Episode 5: Swagger Jacker'''\n\n*After the previous fight, Diego Brandão called out Steven Siler, wanting to fight him next.\n*Miller devised a plan to throw off Team Bisping by postponing the predetermined matches by one week.\n*Josh Ferguson was upset that Miller had given Dustin Neace a cowboy hat with the letter \"B\" for Neace's nickname \"Beast\" on it. Thinking this was to mock the \"B\" on his cowboy hat, calling Neace a \"Swagger Jacker,\" Ferguson took the hat and hid it.\n*The prank rivalry between Neace and Akira Corassani turned ugly when Neace accused Corassani of taking his cowboy hat and trashed Team Bisping's room in retaliation.\n*The featherweight match up was announced: Neace vs. Corassani.\n*During the fight announcement, things heated up even more between Neace and Corassani when the Team Miller member shot in for a takedown, causing a scuffle on the mat with both coaching teams coming to break it up.\n*Miller confronted team snitch John Dodson, telling him his actions caused all the drama at the fight announcements. Dodson showed no remorse, claiming that \"shit happens.\"\n*'''Akira Corassani''' defeated Dustin Neace via majority decision after 2 rounds.\n*Although referee [[Herb Dean]] did not stop the fight, Team Miller questioned the outcome when Corassani looked to be tapped out while caught in Neace's heel hook in the first round.\n*Bisping was relieved that his team finally has a win over the orange team and enjoys the victory by watching Miller's car get towed after accidentally parking in a handicap space.\n\n'''Episode 6: Thrown to the Lions'''\n\n*The first fight is between featherweights Steven Siler and Diego Brandão.\n*'''Diego Brandão''' defeated Steven Siler via knock out (punches) at 0:30 of round 1.\n*After Team Mayhem's training session, the coaches noticed that Roland Delorme's right foot was swollen; thinking it is a staph infection, Delorme was rushed to the emergency room.\n*Before the fighter announcements, Bisping made a speech about how Team Miller should be \"gracious losers\" after they lose a fight.\n*The bantamweight matchup was announced: John Albert vs. John Dodson.\n*The last bantamweight matchup was also announced: T.J. Dillashaw vs. Roland Delorme.\n*Dillashaw's teammates thought he took the coward's way out by dodging the harder fight with Dodson and taking the easier fight with Delorme, having Albert fight in his place instead; claiming that Albert was being \"thrown to the lions.\"\n*'''John Dodson''' defeated John Albert via unanimous decision after 2 rounds.\n\n'''Episode 7: Draft Dodger'''\n\n*Roland Delorme's foot injury left him questioning his future fighting on the show.\n*Former [[Brazil]]ian [[muay thai]] champion [[Rafael Cordeiro]] of ''[[Chute Boxe Academy]]'' guest coached during Team Miller's training session.\n*UFC legend [[Tito Ortiz]] made a surprise appearance to guest coach Team Bisping, teaching them the art of ground and pound.\n*Dillashaw – referred to as the \"Draft Dodger\" by his teammates – had both Akira Corassani and Diego Brandão give him a hard time in training. After taking hard shots from Corassani in his sparring session, Dillashaw took out his frustration on Marcus Brimage, escalating it into an arguing match.\n*The doctor cleared Delorme of his foot infection to fight in his upcoming match.\n*Miller pulled a prank on Team Bisping coach [[Tiki Ghosn]] by surrounding his truck with concrete highway barricades in his parking space so he couldn't get out.\n*'''T.J. Dillashaw''' defeated Roland Delorme via submission (rear-naked choke) at 1:43 of round 2.\n*After the preliminary fights, Miller had five fighters moving on and Bisping has three.\n*White announced the semifinal matchups: Bantamweight fights: T.J. Dillashaw vs. Dustin Pague and John Dodson vs. Bedford. Featherweight fights: Akira Corassani vs. Dennis Bermudez and Diego Brandão vs. Bryan Caraway.\n\n'''Episode 8: Don't Do This at Home, Kids'''\n\n*Louis Gaudinot broke down the separation going on in the house, breaking it down into three groups: Himself, John Dodson, Josh Ferguson, Diego Brandão, Marcus Brimage, and Akira Corassani all hang out with each other; then there's the \"Bible Study Group\" which is Johnny Bedford, Dennis Bermudez, and Dustin Pague who stay to themselves; and lastly is the \"Casino\" made up of T.J. Dillashaw, Dustin Neace, Prince (John Albert), Bryan Caraway, and Stephen Bass who all play cards together every night.\n*Corassani was sick of Caraway always doing his hair in the morning and decided to take an electric razor and shave a chunk of his hair off while he was sleeping, which escalated into Team Miller having to barricade their doors at nighttime.\n*Bisping and Tiki Ghosn played a prank against the orange team by pulling off what they thought was the best prank of the season; spraying them with fire extinguishers in their locker room while a live mariachi band plays as they were trying to escape the fumes.\n*Afghan-Dutch kickboxing champion [[Siyar Bahadurzada]] who trains with the ''[[Golden Glory]]'' team in the Netherlands guest coached during Team Miller's training session and gets Bermudez ready for his fight by simulating Corassani's stand-up style.\n*Before their featherweight semifinal match, Corassani tested Bermudez' patience by drawing a picture of him as an owl mocking his unintelligent \"quotes of the day\" and then singing a song about how he was going to be victorious over Bermudez in what he called a \"slaughter.\"\n*'''Dennis Bermudez''' defeated Akira Corassani via submission (guillotine choke) at 3:12 of round 1.\n*Corassani did not take his loss well, initially disbelieving it in his post-fight groggy state; meanwhile, Bermudez celebrates moving on to the final.\n\n'''Episode 9: Get Me to the Finals'''\n\n*Dustin Pague acted like a good Samaritan when he told the van driver to stop so he could give a homeless man on the street a food care package.\n*When Miller stopped by the house for the fighters' barbecue, he dared Pague to eat a couple of live backyard bugs. Pague accepted and ate them for Miller's $60.\n*Bisping and Miller went head-to-head in the annual \"Coaches Challenge\" when they played a game of [[Air Hockey]] for $10,000 cash ($1,500 each member of the winning team) on the roof of [[Palms Casino Resort|The Palms]].\n*Even though Bisping rose to victory against Miller, three games to one, the orange team gets a laugh when, after his victory dance on the Air Hockey table, Bisping slips on his backside the moment he jumps off the table and lands back on the floor.\n*Second semifinal fight took place (bantamweights) with Dillashaw matched up against Pague.\n*'''T.J. Dillashaw''' defeated Dustin Pague via unanimous decision (30-26, 30–27, 30–26) after 3 rounds, to move onto the finale.\n\n'''Episode 10: It's About to Go Down'''\n\n*The last two semifinal fights took place with the bantamweights having Johnny Bedford vs. John Dodson and featherweights having Diego Brandão vs. Bryan Caraway.\n*'''John Dodson''' defeated Johnny Bedford via knock out (punches) at 1:00 of round 2, to move onto the finale.\n*After the fight, Bedford was devastated and shocked that Dodson actually had the power to knock him out.\n*Since it was the last day in the house, the guys threw a party and Miller crashed it with his coaches and holds a [[beer pong]] match and does stunts with his [[BMX bike]] into the pool.\n*Like with his previous fights, Caraway was nervous about his upcoming fight and once again started to doubt his martial arts abilities.\n*'''Diego Brandão''' defeated Bryan Caraway via technical knock out (punches) at 4:15 of round 1, to move onto the finale.\n*Even though Caraway lost, he gained White's respect by having heart in the cage after taking a full minute of Brandao's shots.\n*After the last fight, White calls for a pose down between Bisping and Miller, hyping their fight for the finale.\n\n==Featherweight Bracket==\n{{Round16\n\n\n||'''Steven Siler'''|'''SUB'''|Micah Miller|3\n||'''Diego Brandao'''|'''KO'''|Jesse
